Hi, doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me today, I really appreciate your time. 

Um, well, I guess I'm here because... well, I've been having a few little slip-ups lately. It's probably nothing major, I mean, I'm doing okay overall! But I keep forgetting things that I really shouldn’t. Just silly mistakes, really. Like, I'll forget to call the clinic back, or... well, I actually missed a heart follow-up appointment recently, which I know I shouldn't do. And if I'm doing a little repair job around the house, I'll show up and realize I didn't bring the right tools with me. 

I'm a bit embarrassed to even mention it, to be honest, because I know I should be on top of these things. It's not a huge disaster or anything, I just want to make sure I'm taking care of myself properly. Does that make sense? I hope I'm giving you the right kind of information to start with.

Oh, well, um... I really do try my best to pay attention, I promise I do. I don't want you to think I'm just ignoring people or not caring about what they're saying! 

But... I suppose, maybe just a little bit, it has gotten a touch harder to stay completely focused. Like, I can sit down at the bridge table with my friends, or go to one of our Fix-It Friends planning meetings, and I have every intention of paying close attention. I really do! But then... after a little while, my mind just sort of slides off somewhere else. And before I know it, I realize I’ve missed half of what was just said. 

I feel so terrible when it happens, because I really respect the people I'm with and I want to be involved. It's just... a little harder to keep my thoughts from drifting off than it used to be. I hope that makes sense? I'm not trying to be difficult or anything, just being honest about those little moments. Is that what you meant by staying focused?

It’s just... you know, the day-to-day stuff. Just those silly little schedule slip-ups I mentioned, like the appointments or forgetting a tool. I really hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is, because it's honestly just a bit of scatterbrained behavior on my part. \n\nDoes that answer your question okay? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel confused about the date, time, or where you are, or find yourself unsure about simple decisions or directions that you used to handle easily?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. No, nothing like that. I... I always know what day it is, and I certainly know where I am! I promise I'm not getting lost or, you know, confused about directions or anything. I can still find my way around just fine, and making simple decisions is really no trouble at all. \n\nI don't want you to worry about me in that regard, doctor. Like I was saying, it's really just those silly little slips with my schedule or forgetting a wrench, not... not any sort of real confusion. I'm still doing okay with all the normal day-to-day thinking. \n\nIs that... is that the kind of information you needed? I just want to be sure I'm giving you exactly what you're looking for.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your ability to manage your usual responsibilities at work or home, such as handling finances, following recipes, or completing projects, that weren’t a problem before?\n\nPatient: Well... I really do try my best to pay attention, I promise I do. I don't want you to think I'm just ignoring people or not caring about what they're saying! \n\nBut... I suppose, maybe just a little bit, it has gotten a touch harder to stay completely focused. Like, I can sit down at the bridge table with my friends, or go to one of our Fix-It Friends planning meetings, and I have every intention of paying close attention. I really do! But then... after a little while, my mind just sort of slides off somewhere else. Well, um... I really hope it doesn't sound like I'm complaining. I know folks go through much worse, and I'm very grateful for my health! But... if I'm being perfectly honest, I suppose it's been causing real trouble for about a year and two months now. Give or take a little? 

You see, I had a bit of a cardiac scare back then. Oh, I'm doing just fine now, I promise! But... well, it forced me to cut back on all the walking and the hands-on projects I used to do. Those things always helped keep me settled, you know? Kept my hands and feet busy. And since I haven't been able to do them as much... that's when these little hiccups really started getting more noticeable. 

I hope it's alright that I mentioned the heart thing? I just wanted to give you the full picture of when this all started, since you asked about the timing. I'm doing my best to manage it, I really am. Does that timeline help you at all?

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... I really don't want you to think poorly of me, or that I'm becoming a burden to anyone. I try so hard to be helpful, I really do! But... I suppose, if I'm being completely honest, a few folks have mentioned things. I hope that doesn't make me sound terrible.

It's just... well, it's gotten to the point where the guys at the senior center repair group, the Fix-It Friends... they can't quite count on me the way they used to. I'll get there and realize I've forgotten my tools, or I'll be so restless I can't stay focused on the project we're doing. And... oh dear, I'm a bit embarrassed to admit this, but my doctor's office has actually had to call and chase me down about missing some of my heart follow-up appointments. I just get so sidetracked and the time gets away from me! 

I really don't mean to be forgetful or let anyone down. I hope you know I'm trying my best to stay on top of things. Does that... does that make sense? I'm so sorry if that's not what you were hoping to hear, I just want to make sure I'm answering you properly.

I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel confused about the date, time, or where you are, or find yourself unsure about simple decisions or directions that you used to handle easily?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. No, nothing like that. I... I always know what day it is, and I certainly know where I am! I promise I'm not getting lost or, you know, confused about directions or anything. I can still find my way around just fine, and making simple decisions is really no trouble at all. \n\nI don't want you to worry about me in that regard, doctor. I hope I'm answering your questions right.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it harder than before to focus on tasks, follow through on instructions, or stay organized when you’re working on something that requires attention?\n\nPatient: Oh, well, um... I really do try my best to pay attention, I promise I do. I don't want you to think I'm just ignoring people or not caring about what they're saying! \n\nBut... I suppose, maybe just a little bit, it has gotten a touch harder to stay completely focused. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... I hope this doesn't make me sound too bothersome to be around, but... yes, I suppose I do fidget a fair bit. I try very hard to sit quietly, I really do! Especially when I'm supposed to be listening. 

But sometimes, without even realizing it, my foot will just start going, or my fingers will be tapping away on the table. Or I'll find myself just sort of turning a pen around and around in my hand. I usually don't even notice I'm doing it until someone politely points it out to me. 

I'm so sorry if that sounds a bit childish for a man my age. I really don't mean to be a distraction to anyone else. It's just a little nervous energy, I think! I hope that's an alright answer? I just want to be sure I'm giving you the right information.

Oh, dear... I, um, I hope I haven't been talking your ear off too much today! I try very hard to be a good listener, I really do. But... well, yes, I suppose I can get a bit carried away with my words sometimes.

I suppose, maybe just a little bit, it has gotten a touch harder to stay completely focused. Like, I can sit down at the bridge table with my friends, or go to one of our Fix-It Friends planning meetings, and I have every intention of paying close attention. I really do! But then... after a little while, my mind just sort of slides off somewhere else. Oh, my... well, if I'm being perfectly honest... I suppose I did have a bit of trouble with that, yes. I hope it doesn't paint me as a troublemaker, because I really did want to be a good student for my teachers!

I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel confused about the date, time, or where you are, or find yourself unsure about simple decisions or directions that you used to handle easily?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. No, nothing like that. I... I always know what day it is, and I certainly know where I am! I promise I'm not getting lost or, you know, confused about directions or anything. I can still find my way around just fine, and making simple decisions is really no trouble at all. \n\nI don't want you to worry about me in that regard, doctor. Like I was saying, it's really just those silly little slips with my schedule or forgetting a wrench, not... not any sort of real confusion. I'm still doing okay with all the normal day-to-day thinking. \n\nIs that... is that the kind of information you needed? I just want to be sure I'm giving you exactly what you're looking for.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your ability to manage your usual responsibilities at work or home, such as handling finances, following recipes, or completing projects, that weren’t a problem before?\n\nPatient: Well... I'm doing just fine with my bills and cooking and all of that, I promise! No problems there at all. But, um... I guess when it comes to projects and my usual routine, there has been a tiny bit of a change. \n\nI hate to even admit it, to be perfectly honest, because I've always prided myself on being reliable. But... well, it’s gotten to where the senior-center repair group... they can’t really count on me the way they used to. I just keep showing up without the right gear or getting the times mixed up. And, like I mentioned earlier, my doctor’s office has actually had to chase me down a bit about those missed heart follow-ups. \n\nI feel just awful about it, making the nurses call me and being a letdown to the guys at the center. I really am trying to stay on top of things, I don't want you to think I'm just ignoring my responsibilities! It's just a few little hiccups. Is... is that the sort of example you were looking for? I hope I'm answering your questions right.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it harder than before to focus on tasks, follow through on instructions, or stay organized when you’re working on something that requires attention?\n\nPatient: Oh, well, um... I really do try my best to pay attention, I promise I do. I don't want you to think I'm just ignoring people or not caring about what they're saying! \n\nBut... I suppose, maybe just a little bit, it has gotten a touch harder to stay completely focused. Like, I can sit down at the bridge table with my friends, or go to one of our Fix-It Friends planning meetings, and I have every intention of paying close attention. I really do! But then... after a little while, my mind just sort of slides off somewhere else. And before I know it, I realize I’ve missed half of what was just said. \n\nI feel so terrible when it happens, because I really respect the people I'm with and I want to be involved. It's just... a little harder to keep my thoughts from drifting off than it used to be. I hope that makes sense? I'm not trying to be difficult or anything, just being honest about those little moments. Is that what you meant by staying focused?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your mood lately, like feeling more down, hopeless, or less interested in activities you usually enjoy, such as bridge or the Fix-It Friends meetings?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, no. Nothing like that at all. I really don't want you to think I'm sitting around moping or feeling sorry for myself! I'm generally a very happy person, I promise. \n\nI still love going to bridge, and I definitely still want to be at the Fix-It Friends meetings. I haven't lost interest in any of that. If anything, I'm just as eager to go as I always was! I certainly don't feel down or hopeless. Life is good, you know? I'm very blessed. \n\nI just... I just have those little moments of drifting off or forgetting a tool, like we talked about. But my mood is just fine. I hope it's okay that I don't have any of those sad feelings to report? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture of how I'm doing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your memory or thinking has slowed down or isn't as sharp as it used to be, even if it's just a little?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no... I really don't think so. I mean, I certainly hope you don't think my mind is going or anything like that! I'd hate for you to worry about my memory in that way. To be perfectly honest, I still feel quite sharp. \n\nIt's really just those little hiccups we talked about—getting a bit sidetracked or rushing out the door and forgetting my gear. But my actual thinking? No, it hasn't slowed down at all. When I'm working on a repair with the guys or playing a hand of bridge, my brain works just as fast as it always did. \n\nI really do try to keep my mind active and stay on the ball. I hope that's a good sign? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information and not making things sound worse than they actually are. Am I doing okay with these questions?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed these memory or attention changes getting worse over time, or have they stayed about the same since you first noticed them?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really hope it doesn't sound like I'm complaining. I know folks go through much worse, and I'm very grateful for my health! But... if I'm being perfectly honest, I suppose it's been causing real trouble for about a year and two months now. Give or take a little? \n\nYou see, I had a bit of a cardiac scare back then. Oh, I'm doing just fine now, I promise! But... well, it forced me to cut back on all the walking and the hands-on projects I used to do. Those things always helped keep me settled, you know? Kept my hands and feet busy. And since I haven't been able to do them as much... that's when these little hiccups really started getting more noticeable. \n\nI hope it's alright that I mentioned the heart thing? I just wanted to give you the full picture of when this all started, since you asked about the timing. I'm doing my best to manage it, I really am. Does that timeline help you at all?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any difficulty learning new things or following along with tasks that you used to be able to do easily, even if it's something small?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say that at all. I really hope you don't think I'm losing my touch or anything! I can still learn new things just fine, I promise. \n\nLike with the Fix-It Friends, if someone shows me a new way to wire a lamp or a different technique for sanding, I pick it up right away. I'm still very capable of following along with all of that. I really don't want to give you the impression that I'm struggling to comprehend things or that I'm getting confused. \n\nIt's really just the... the fidgeting and the getting sidetracked that we talked about. But as far as learning or following along with tasks? Oh, I'm still doing very well with all of that. I hope that's the kind of answer you were looking for? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right idea about how my brain is working. I'm doing okay, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: Has anyone close to you, like family, friends, or your doctor, ever mentioned that you seem more forgetful or distracted than before?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really don't want you to think poorly of me, or that I'm becoming a burden to anyone. I try so hard to be helpful, I really do! But... I suppose, if I'm being completely honest, a few folks have mentioned things. I hope that doesn't make me sound terrible.\n\nIt's just... well, it's gotten to the point where the guys at the senior center repair group, the Fix-It Friends... they can't quite count on me the way they used to. I'll get there and realize I've forgotten my tools, or I'll be so restless I can't stay focused on the project we're doing. And... oh dear, I'm a bit embarrassed to admit this, but my doctor's office has actually had to call and chase me down about missing some of my heart follow-up appointments. I just get so sidetracked and the time gets away from me! \n\nI really don't mean to be forgetful or let anyone down. I hope you know I'm trying my best to stay on top of things. Does that... does that make sense? I'm so sorry if that's not what you were hoping to hear, I just want to make sure I'm answering you properly.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had anyone sit down with you and formally test your memory or thinking skills, like during a medical evaluation or at the senior center?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I don't believe so. I've never had to do a formal test like that. I really hope that's alright! I suppose my regular doctor hasn't felt things were quite bad enough to need a big, official evaluation like that, which is a blessing, really. \n\nI just wouldn't want you to think I've been hiding test results from you or anything like that. I always try to be an open book! But no, no one at the senior center or at the clinic has ever sat me down with a pencil and paper for my memory. \n\nIs... is that something I should have done by now? I can certainly ask my doctor about it next time if you think it's necessary. I just want to make sure I'm doing exactly what I'm supposed to be doing. I hope it's okay that I don't have any test scores to give you?\n\nDoctor: Do you yourself feel like your memory or ability to focus has gotten worse compared to before, even if just slightly?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I really don't think so. I mean, I hope I'm not giving you the impression that my mind is slipping! I really try to stay sharp. \n\nI don't feel like my memory itself is any worse than it was, say, ten years ago. It's just that I'm always up and moving, you know? My mind is always racing to the next thing, so maybe I don't always pay attention the way I should in the first place. But if I actually stop and focus on something, I can remember it just fine! \n\nI really don't feel like I'm losing my edge or anything like that. I hope that's a good thing to hear? I just wouldn't want you to worry that things are worse than they really are. I'm doing pretty well for my age, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or find yourself fidgeting with your hands or feet, especially when you're trying to sit still or focus on something?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I hope this doesn't make me sound too bothersome to be around, but... yes, I suppose I do fidget a fair bit. I try very hard to sit quietly, I really do! Especially when I'm supposed to be listening. \n\nBut sometimes, without even realizing it, my foot will just start going, or my fingers will be tapping away on the table. Or I'll find myself just sort of turning a pen around and around in my hand. I usually don't even notice I'm doing it until someone politely points it out to me. \n\nI'm so sorry if that sounds a bit childish for a man my age. I really don't mean to be a distraction to anyone else. It's just a little nervous energy, I think! I hope that's an alright answer? I just want to be sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel unusually drowsy or have trouble staying awake during the day, even if you’ve had enough sleep the night before?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. Um... I hope it's alright to say, but I really don't have any trouble staying awake at all. If anything, I'm usually the one who has a bit too much energy during the day! \n\nI know some folks my age like to take a nice afternoon nap, and I suppose there's absolutely nothing wrong with that, but... well, I just can't seem to lay down long enough for one. I'm always up and doing things around the house or puttering out in the yard. I hope that's a good thing? \n\nI certainly wouldn't want you to think I'm sluggish or not taking care of myself properly. As long as I get my rest at night, I'm wide awake and ready to go all day long. Is... is that the sort of answer you were looking for? I just want to make sure I'm giving you a clear picture of how I'm doing.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself talking more than usual, or feeling like you just can’t sit still and need to keep moving or doing something, even when it might not be appropriate?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear... I, um, I hope I haven't been talking your ear off too much today! I try very hard to be a good listener, I really do. But... well, yes, I suppose I can get a bit carried away with my words sometimes. \n\nLike, if I'm down at the center, and I get started explaining a repair plan for a project, or maybe I'm just telling a story... I get so enthusiastic, and before I know it, I’ve just talked right over the other fellows. I think I end up wearing them out a bit, to be honest. \n\nI always feel just awful about it when I realize what I've done. I certainly don't mean to be rude or dominate the conversation! I just get so caught up in the moment, and my motor just keeps running, you know? I hope that doesn't make me sound too overbearing... I just enjoy sharing things with people. Does... does that answer your question alright?\n\nDoctor: Looking back, did you ever have trouble sitting still or staying focused when you were a child, even if it was just a little?\n\nPatient: Oh, my... well, if I'm being perfectly honest... I suppose I did have a bit of trouble with that, yes. I hope it doesn't paint me as a troublemaker, because I really did want to be a good student for my teachers! \n\nBut, um... even way back when I was just a boy in grade school, I was always the kid who just couldn't quite manage to stay in his seat. I'd get so eager, you know, that I'd just blurt out the answers before anyone else had a chance to raise their hand. And... well, I hate to admit it, but I did get a fair few notes sent home to my parents for talking too much during lessons. \n\nI always felt terrible when my mother had to read those notes. I certainly didn't mean to be disobedient! I just had so much energy and wanted to be part of everything going on in the room. I hope you don't judge me too harshly for that! I was just a very spirited little boy, I think. Is... is that the kind of history you were looking for? I just want to be sure I'm answering correctly.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, that's a very good question... well, like I mentioned before, I suppose I've always been a bit, oh, energetic and spirited. But as for it causing this much real trouble for me and everyone else? That's actually fairly recent. 

I'd say it's been about a year and two months now. You see, I had a bit of a cardiac scare back then—I'm doing my absolute best to follow all the doctors' rules now, I promise! But because of it, I had to cut way back on my daily walking and all the little hands-on projects I used to do around the house. 

I didn't quite realize it at the time, but keeping busy with all those projects really used to keep me settled. Without all that activity to sort of burn off the energy... well, things have just gotten much harder to manage. I hope it doesn't sound like I'm just making excuses for being scatterbrained lately! I really am trying my best to behave. Does... does that answer what you were asking?
